I still put on records or try to cook, or mess around with electronics, and I want those things to help. They just don’t really reach me most days. It’s like I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and the things that used to reset me don’t do much anymore. I’m not sure if that’s answering it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A few, sort of. I still try putting on records, or cooking something simple, or tinkering with electronics… and every now and then there’s a small moment where it’s okay. But most days I wake up with that flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me the way they used to. I’m not sure if that counts as still enjoying them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel a more significant loss of pleasure in activities than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, most days are kind of the same in that I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there. But some days it’s more noticeable, like even if I put on a record or try to cook something or mess with a little electronics project, it just… doesn’t reach me at all.\n\nOther days maybe there’s a tiny bit of relief, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yeah, there are days where the loss of pleasure feels stronger. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is this flat, heavy sadness something you feel every day?\n\nPatient: Pretty much, yeah… I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, that flat, heavy sadness. I still try to do the normal things—put on a record, cook a little, tinker with electronics—but a lot of the time they just don’t reach me like they used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and two months where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I don’t think I’ve felt completely back to normal. I guess I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… not fully. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Is this flat, heavy sadness something that stays with you for most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s more like it stays with me, most of the day. I usually wake up with it already there, and even when I try to do things that used to kind of reset me—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with electronics—it doesn’t really lift much.\n\nI don’t mean it’s always at the exact same level every minute, but it’s there in the background a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I still do sleep some, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s not like it used to be.\n\nI’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just sort of listening to noises in the apartment, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn already tense. I don’t feel rested, really… more like I never fully powered down. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: A few things, sort of… I still try to put on a record sometimes, or cook something simple, or tinker with electronics, because those used to help me feel like myself. I’m not sure if that’s answering it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A few, sort of. I still try putting on records, or cooking something simple, or tinkering with electronics… and every now and then there’s a small moment where it’s okay. But most days I wake up with that flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me the way they used to. I’m not sure if that counts as still enjoying them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any days where you feel a more significant loss of pleasure in activities than others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, most days are kind of the same in that I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there. But some days it’s more noticeable, like even if I put on a record or try to cook something or mess with a little electronics project, it just… doesn’t reach me at all.\n\nOther days maybe there’s a tiny bit of relief, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But yeah, there are days where the loss of pleasure feels stronger. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is this flat, heavy sadness something you feel every day?\n\nPatient: Pretty much, yeah… I don’t want to overstate it, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, that flat, heavy sadness. I still try to do the normal things—put on a record, cook a little, tinker with electronics—but a lot of the time they just don’t reach me like they used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and two months where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I don’t think I’ve felt completely back to normal. It’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment and all my routines kind of fell apart. I guess I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… not fully. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Is this flat, heavy sadness something that stays with you for most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s more like it stays with me, most of the day. I usually wake up with it already there, and even when I try to do things that used to kind of reset me—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with electronics—it doesn’t really lift much.\n\nI don’t mean it’s always at the exact same level every minute, but it’s there in the background a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I still do sleep some, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s not like it used to be.\n\nI’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just sort of listening to noises in the apartment, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn already tense. I don’t feel rested, really… more like I never fully powered down. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: A few things, sort of… I still try to put on a record sometimes, or cook something simple, or tinker with electronics, because those used to help me feel like myself. But lately it’s like they don’t really reach me the same way.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and even if I do those things, I’m not really looking forward to them like I used to. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’ve really noticed, anyway. My clothes seem to fit about the same.\n\nI haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer, but there hasn’t been some big change that I’m aware of. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you have no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still sometimes go through the motions, but things like putting on a record, cooking something, messing with electronics… those used to kind of reset me.\n\nNow most days I wake up already feeling this flat, heavy sadness, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. It’s not like I hate them, exactly. It’s more like there’s no pleasure in them the way there used to be. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, I’m still functioning, I guess. I’m still going to work, so I don’t want to make it sound like everything has fallen apart.

Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year and two months where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had better days here and there, but I don’t think I’ve felt completely back to normal. It’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment and all my routines kind of fell apart. I guess I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, but… not fully. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Is this flat, heavy sadness something that stays with you for most of the day, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s more like it stays with me, most of the day. I usually wake up with it already there, and even when I try to do things that used to kind of reset me—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with electronics—it doesn’t really lift much.\n\nI don’t mean it’s always at the exact same level every minute, but it’s there in the background a lot. Is that answering it the right way?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I still do sleep some, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s not like it used to be.\n\nI’ll lie there for hours sometimes, just sort of listening to noises in the apartment, and then when I finally do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn already tense. I don’t feel rested, really… more like I never fully powered down. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in and look forward to doing?\n\nPatient: A few things, sort of… I still try to put on a record sometimes, or cook something simple, or tinker with electronics, because those used to help me feel like myself. But lately it’s like they don’t really reach me the same way.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and even if I do those things, I’m not really looking forward to them like I used to. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not anything I’ve really noticed, anyway. My clothes seem to fit about the same.\n\nI haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I don’t want to give you the wrong answer, but there hasn’t been some big change that I’m aware of. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy and now find you have no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still sometimes go through the motions, but things like putting on a record, cooking something, messing with electronics… those used to kind of reset me.\n\nNow most days I wake up already feeling this flat, heavy sadness, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. It’s not like I hate them, exactly. It’s more like there’s no pleasure in them the way there used to be. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more where you felt this flat, heavy sadness nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has been at least that long. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness.\n\nAnd even when I try to do the things that used to help—put on a record, cook something, tinker with electronics—it doesn’t really reach me anymore. So… yeah, nearly every day, I’d say. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but now find you have no interest in at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I kind of said this, but yes. I used to get at least some interest out of putting on a record, cooking something, or tinkering with electronics.\n\nLately it’s more like… I wake up with that same flat, heavy sadness, and those things don’t really reach me. I might still try them, but the interest or pleasure just isn’t there the way it used to be. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but now find you have no pleasure in at all, every single day?\n\nPatient: Every single day… I’m a little hesitant to say it that strongly, but yeah, most days it feels that way. I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and the things that used to kind of reset me—putting on a record, cooking, tinkering with electronics—they just don’t really reach me anymore.\n\nI can still do them sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to, but the pleasure isn’t really there. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… but honestly not much. I don’t want to say there’s absolutely nothing, because that sounds so stark, but most days I wake up with that same flat, heavy sadness, and the things that used to kind of reset me—like putting on a record, cooking, messing with electronics—just don’t really reach me anymore.\n\nSo… I guess I still try them sometimes, but pleasure is pretty hard to find in them right now. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any suicidal thoughts or feelings of wanting to harm yourself since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t think I’d hurt myself. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, I guess I have had thoughts where it feels like it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. Like nobody would have to deal with me anymore. I’m sorry, I know that sounds bad—I’m not trying to be dramatic. That’s just where my mind goes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s hard to admit it that plainly, but this has been going on a lot longer than two weeks.\n\nIt’s been about a year and two months, really—since that pipe burst forced me into the smaller apartment and my routines kind of fell apart. I kept thinking I’d adjust and it would pass, so I didn’t really want to make a big deal of it, but… yeah, most days it’s been there for most of the day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning due to these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still functioning, I guess. I’m still going to work, so I don’t want to make it sound like everything has fallen apart.\n\nBut it has affected things. At work I’ve had to ask for some accommodations and extra review, because I’ve been making documentation mistakes that I really wouldn’t normally make. That’s… embarrassing to say, honestly.\n\nAnd socially, I’ve been skipping some board-game nights, and I don’t leave the apartment as much. It’s not that I don’t care about people, it just feels harder to keep up with normal routines than it used to. Does that count as impairment?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
